### 7.4 Run history and the dynamic SSA model (CC, R)

The classical environment holds **dynamic instances**: every executed
defining instruction creates a new instance retained in the run history
until reversal; a name denotes its most recent instance. Frames:

| Instruction | Frame |
|---|---|
| any defining instruction | its defined instance |
| `br` | predecessor block, prior block-parameter instances |
| `store` | address, width, overwritten bytes |
| `alloc` | old bump pointer, the table entry written, the zero-filled range |
| `free` | the entry's old live bit |
| `call` | invocation boundary (callee, argument values) |
| `ret` | result values, the callee's activation and the frame link |
| `uncall` | the reversed segment's id (skipped by whole-run reversal, below) |
| `effect` | tape index plus the device-specific record of §7.3; reversal also removes its effect-tape entry |
| `hostcall` | payload bytes and reply bytes |
| `qreg.*`, `measure` | the ledger record of §3.3 (ids, class, widths, prior states, whether a free's cleanliness was verified) and the measured bits |
| `budget.mint` | the token and its tuple; reversal revokes the token |
| `oracle_call` / `action_call` | (region hash, variant, inherited control count, classical args with every token's tuple, register ids): reversed by re-application / adjoint, exactly once, by this frame |
| fault record (a failing instruction, `trap`, `fault`) | cause code (§4.9) and program point; terminal. Reversal (§10) removes the record; the failing instruction had no other effect, so the state is then the state before it |

**Logical history.** Frames and dynamic instances specify the
*logical* history. An executor MAY store it by checkpoints, deltas or
deterministic replay, provided it reconstructs the same logical frames,
instances, reversal marks and traversal order; it never re-issues an
external event (effects are replayed from the effect tape and host
replies from their frames, never requested again), never re-executes a
region call to recover its record, and never copies quantum state. T4 is
stated over the logical history. **Active and audit history.** The
*active* history is the sequence of frames not marked reversed;
reversed frames and `uncall` markers remain as immutable *audit
records*. Undoing a defining frame removes its dynamic instance from the
active environment although its audit record is retained.

**Pure frames.** A frame is **pure** iff it belongs to `br` or to a
defining instruction among `const`, the binops, `udiv..srem`, `icmp`,
`select`, `zext sext trunc`, `ptrtoint inttoptr`, `load`, `qreg.width
qreg.id qreg.from_id`: it changes only the set of instances. Every other
frame (`store alloc free call ret uncall trap fault effect hostcall
qreg.new qreg.free qreg.slice qreg.concat measure budget.mint`, region
calls, fault records) is impure.

**History traversal** (the one reversal procedure, used by segment
`uncall` and by whole-run reversal). A range of frames is **reversed**
by visiting its frames in LIFO order; a frame already marked reversed
and an `uncall` frame are **skipped**; every other frame is undone by
its row of the table above and then marked reversed. Reversed frames
stay in the history, marked, and are never undone again. An `uncall`
marker is always skipped: when a later traversal (an enclosing
segment's `uncall`, or whole-run reversal) reaches it, the frames of the
segment it names are already marked and are skipped too, so neither the
marker nor its segment is undone, re-executed or redone a second time.

**Segment uncall** (`uncall @f (args) (results)`). Let S be the most
recent completed call segment of the history (a `call` frame, its
matching `ret` frame and every frame between them). The `uncall` is

# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.

### 16.9 Example: rung 2 (heap, phis, a loop, `free`)

A linked list of n nodes built with `malloc` (a specialised 16-byte-call witness of the runtimes allocator over `lift.alloc`, shown with
its code; the general C wrapper is specified in §17.1), summed and freed. n comes from a 2-wire `IN` register zero-extended to `i8`:

```llvm lift=listsum region=@listsum desc=IN(2,zext);tgt=8
define ptr @malloc(i64 %n) {
  %p = call ptr @__bennett_nz_alloc(i64 %n)
  ret ptr %p
}

define void @free(ptr %p) {
  %z = icmp eq ptr %p, null
  br i1 %z, label %out, label %rel

rel:
  call void @__bennett_nz_free(ptr %p)
  br label %out

out:
  ret void
}

define internal i8 @listsum(i8 %n) {
b0:
  %z = icmp eq i8 %n, 0
  br i1 %z, label %b3, label %b1

b1:
  %i = phi i8 [ 0, %b0 ], [ %i2, %b1 ]
  %head = phi ptr [ null, %b0 ], [ %node, %b1 ]
  %node = call ptr @malloc(i64 16)
  store i8 %i, ptr %node
  %np = getelementptr i8, ptr %node, i64 8
  store ptr %head, ptr %np
  %i2 = add i8 %i, 1
  %more = icmp ult i8 %i2, %n
  br i1 %more, label %b1, label %b2

b2:
  %p = phi ptr [ %node, %b1 ], [ %next, %b2 ]
  %s = phi i8 [ 0, %b1 ], [ %s2, %b2 ]
  %v = load i8, ptr %p
  %s2 = add i8 %s, %v
  %nextp = getelementptr i8, ptr %p, i64 8
  %next = load ptr, ptr %nextp
  call void @free(ptr %p)
  %end = icmp eq ptr %next, null
  br i1 %end, label %b4, label %b2

b3:

### 18.5 Oracle semantics

An `oracle` event applies, on every basis state of its operands, the permutation |x⟩|t⟩|e⟩ ↦
|x⟩|t ⊕ payload(F_B(x, S))⟩|e ⊕ code(F_B(x, S))⟩ where F_B is the reference interpreter's result on
the reference object of the dispatched shape (§16) with the token's budget, and S is the entry
state of §8.5 (the caller's classical memory at the call). It is an involution (ISA §5.2).

### 18.6 Reversible semantics

`reversible(f, budget, env)` and the whole-program R mode (§19.2) run f on the R tier (ISA §3.1):
its classical effects on memory are those of §18.1, its effects are ISA device models on the tape
(§11.2), its region calls are R-tier region calls (§7.5b), and the run history (ISA §7.4) is
retained, so the whole run is reversible by `bennett_reverse` (ISA §10). A fault ends the run
(§17.6).

### 18.7 Conformance

A plugin and an executor (ISA §1.2) together conform iff, for every module the rules accept or
block, every host input and every sequence of measurement and `observe` outcomes, outside the
executions that reach a blocker point: the probability of that sequence, every classical value
the host observes (return values of device entries, memory they write, console output), every
fault code and every trap equal those of the reference semantics; and the quantum state at every
measurement and at every device-entry return equals the reference state, exactly when the program
uses permutation gates only, and otherwise within the error bound its certificate states (ISA
§12.3). A program that reaches `T-CLEAN` in the reference semantics is erroneous; an executor that

 succeeded in 341ms:
### 18.5 Oracle semantics

An `oracle` event applies, on every basis state of its operands, the permutation |x⟩|t⟩|e⟩ ↦
|x⟩|t ⊕ payload(F_B(x, S))⟩|e ⊕ code(F_B(x, S))⟩ where F_B is the reference interpreter's result on
the reference object of the dispatched shape (§16) with the token's budget, and S is the entry
state of §8.5 (the caller's classical memory at the call). It is an involution (ISA §5.2).

### 18.6 Reversible semantics

`reversible(f, budget, env)` and the whole-program R mode (§19.2) run f on the R tier (ISA §3.1):
its classical effects on memory are those of §18.1, its effects are ISA device models on the tape
(§11.2), its region calls are R-tier region calls (§7.5b), and the run history (ISA §7.4) is
retained, so the whole run is reversible by `bennett_reverse` (ISA §10). A fault ends the run
(§17.6); the `FAILED` run is reversible too, under the same eligibility (ISA K6).

### 18.7 Conformance

A plugin and an executor (ISA §1.2) together conform iff, for every module the rules accept,
every host input and every sequence of measurement and `observe` outcomes: the probability of that sequence, every classical value
the host observes (return values of device entries, memory they write, console output), every
fault code and every trap equal those of the reference semantics; and the quantum state at every
measurement and at every device-entry return equals the reference state, exactly when the program
uses permutation gates only, and otherwise within the error bound its certificate states (ISA
§12.3). A program that reaches `T-CLEAN` in the reference semantics is erroneous; an executor that
cannot test cleanliness (hardware) need not reproduce that trap (ISA §17 item 14).

### 16.9 Example: rung 2 (heap, phis, a loop, `free`)

its code; the general C wrapper is specified in §17.1), summed and freed. n comes from a 2-wire `IN` register zero-extended to `i8`:

```llvm lift=listsum region=@listsum desc=IN(2,zext);tgt=8
define ptr @malloc(i64 %n) {
  %p = call ptr @__bennett_nz_alloc(i64 %n)
  ret ptr %p
}

define void @free(ptr %p) {
  %z = icmp eq ptr %p, null
  br i1 %z, label %out, label %rel

rel:
  call void @__bennett_nz_free(ptr %p)
  br label %out

out:
  ret void
}

define internal i8 @listsum(i8 %n) {
b0:
  %z = icmp eq i8 %n, 0
  br i1 %z, label %b3, label %b1

b1:
  %i = phi i8 [ 0, %b0 ], [ %i2, %b1 ]
  %head = phi ptr [ null, %b0 ], [ %node, %b1 ]
  %node = call ptr @malloc(i64 16)
  store i8 %i, ptr %node
  %np = getelementptr i8, ptr %node, i64 8
  store ptr %head, ptr %np

  %more = icmp ult i8 %i2, %n

b2:
  %p = phi ptr [ %node, %b1 ], [ %next, %b2 ]
  %s = phi i8 [ 0, %b1 ], [ %s2, %b2 ]
  %v = load i8, ptr %p
  %s2 = add i8 %s, %v
  %nextp = getelementptr i8, ptr %p, i64 8
  %next = load ptr, ptr %nextp
  call void @free(ptr %p)
  %end = icmp eq ptr %next, null
  br i1 %end, label %b4, label %b2
